Yesterday's parlays analysis · Tuesday, Nov 10

4 of 13 won · 3 lost by one leg · ROI −4.3%

4 of 13 won, and 3 more lost by a single leg. Unders on high lines won; Over ladders lost to near misses and blowouts.

The three NBA parlays built from Unders ("No" on high lines) won 2 of 3, and their legs hit 96% of the time against the 90% their prices implied. The seven NBA Over ladders won 1 of 7: their legs hit 79% against an implied 87%, and 13 of the day's 15 missed legs fell short by 2 or less. Women's Champions League went 1 for 3, with Chelsea's 0-0 draw at Häcken the only miss in both losing slips.

What went wrong

  • Over legs underperformed their prices: 72 Yes legs hit 79% vs an implied 87%. Missing by 1–2 is normal variance on 80–90¢ legs, and a 10-leg ladder of them makes about 3 near misses likely.
  • Blowouts killed the Overs: 12 of the 13 missed NBA Over legs came from games decided by 20+ (BOS–PHI by 22, PHX–LAC by 23, NY–MIL by 32, POR–OKC by 20). Starters in a rout lose fourth-quarter minutes.
  • The same leg sat in several slips: Jaylen Brown 15+ points sank two parlays, and Chelsea to win sank two more. When a repeated leg misses, every slip holding it goes down together.
  • Two legs on one player double up the risk: Zach LaVine's 6 threes and 31 points beat two Unders in one slip, and SGA missed both his points and assists lines.
  • Low assist and rebound lines for role players (Jaxson Hayes 4+ reb, Paul George 2+ ast, Derrick White 4+ ast) priced at 80–90¢ were closer to coin flips.

What worked

  • Unders on high lines: 52 No legs hit 96% (vs 90% implied), and 2 of 3 of those parlays cashed even at 11 and 20 legs.
  • Close games for Over ladders: the one Over parlay that won (Heat–Jazz) came from a 6-point game where starters played full minutes.
  • The trimmed Women's Champions League slip (no Chelsea) won, while the two that kept Chelsea both lost by that one leg.

For next time

  • Keep building the high-line Under parlays, and use at most one leg per player (no stacking a scorer's points and threes).
  • For Over ladders, skip starters on teams with large spreads, especially on the underdog side; prefer games with small spreads.
  • Don't repeat one leg across several slips. If a leg is in two parlays, the second one adds risk without spreading it.
  • Avoid low assist and rebound lines for players whose role is scoring or bench minutes; they miss by one far more often than the price suggests.
